Before this it sat in `audit_log.after_state` of the `entitlement.%` rows, and every role of a school reads its own audit rows (`audit_read_own_school`), so a principal or a teacher could read it with one query. | **Enforced in the database, both options together, because each alone leaves a gap** (migration `c124_founder_reasons`): **(1)** a founder-only table `entitlement_change_reasons` (one row per audit row; all grants revoked from `public`, `anon`, `authenticated`, `service_role`, `app_backend`, `app_user`; `nca_super` holds SELECT and INSERT only, so it is append-only for the founder too; RLS forced, policies scoped `to nca_super`). A school role fails with "permission denied" whatever tenant or role it claims. **(2)** A RESTRICTIVE select policy `audit_founder_reason_hidden` on `audit_log` (no `super_admin` clause; the founder reads as `nca_super`, BYPASSRLS) hides every `entitlement.%` row whose `before_state` or `after_state` mentions a reason at any depth and in any letter case (matched as text; hiding one row too many is the safe mistake): the older rows are copied to the new table, never edited, because the audit log is append-only. A table alone would have left those rows readable; a policy alone would have hidden the whole event from the school and left the reason one policy away from a tenant table. **(3)** `audit_hash_chain` becomes SECURITY DEFINER (same body): it looked up the previous hash under the caller's RLS, so any row hidden from the caller would have forked the chain. This was already true for platform rows (no school) written by a school role; new rows now chain correctly, while the platform rows already written that way keep their `prev_hash` of `0` (append-only, not edited). The migration stops if the function's owner does not bypass row level security, because the table has FORCE RLS. No audit row is updated or deleted; no grant on `audit_log` changes; the hash inputs are untouched. **Rollout order does not matter:** a check that refused a reason on new audit rows was written and then removed, because the deployed code (which still writes the reason into `after_state`) would have failed plan changes and school approvals between applying the migration and merging, and again on any rollback. Such a row is hidden instead, so the founder's reasons are protected from the moment the migration is applied. **Code:** `writeEntitlementAudit` writes the audit row and its reason in one statement; `entitlementHistory` reads the reason from the new table (so it fails for a school role), falling back to an old-style row's own value for the founder; schools still see that a plan, module or access mode changed and when, never why. The Control form now says "only you can read it; the school never sees it". **Proof:** `test/frameworks/founder-reasons.test.ts` (9 on PGlite, school reads as `app_user`): all five school roles, `anon`, `authenticated`, `app_backend`, and `app_user` claiming `super_admin` are refused on the table; a school's own entitlement rows contain no reason; the older row is hidden by every route while the school's own non-entitlement row with a reason stays; the founder reads all reasons, the older one included; a row written the old way is hidden from the school and still shown to the founder; both tables refuse UPDATE and DELETE for the founder too; the chain of both schools and of the platform rows verifies link by link after a school role writes behind a row it cannot see (this test fails without part 3). `check_rls_policies.py` requires the RESTRICTIVE policy; `check_append_only_grants.py` covers the new table (its self-test, stale since C112, now follows the table list). **Live (2026-10-05, after PR #51 was opened):** applied to the test database first (recorded as `20261005155446`, the file's name), then production (`20261005155606`). On the test database, as `app_user` in a transaction that rolls back: with the school's newest audit row an entitlement row carrying a reason, each of principal, school admin, teacher, accountant, coordinator, and `app_user` claiming `super_admin`, saw 0 entitlement or reason rows and was denied on `entitlement_change_reasons`; a principal's audit insert chained to the hidden row's hash. The 2 older reasons were copied (production has none). The chain had 0 breaks before. Catalog snapshot after: both databases identical in 8 categories (623 columns, 126 policies, 1,937 grants, 11 functions, 4 triggers, 318 constraints, 171 indexes, 64 RLS flags); on both, only `nca_super` holds a grant on the new table (SELECT, INSERT). An independent review of the diff found no leak and no breakage; three of its points were taken (the owner guard, the wider match, the wording on older platform rows). Known and unchanged: the chain is ordered by time alone, and `check_append_only_grants.py` does not read `grant all`. |

| **C126** | PR-4a (supervisor, 2026-10-05): Raise Ticket with a founder queue: a status machine with reopen, every transition audited, screenshots behind `FileStorage` (school-scoped, RLS), the school sees only its own tickets and the founder all. NetCyberAI makes the working moves; a school may confirm a resolved ticket closed, reopen a resolved or closed one, and its reply to a ticket waiting on it moves the ticket back to in progress (by replying only: it cannot set that move without answering). **Enforced twice:** in the API, and by a trigger on `support_tickets` that refuses any other move, for the founder and for a school session alike (the test tries all 40 pairs by raw SQL). **Data** (migration `p4_support_tickets`): `support_tickets`, `support_ticket_messages`, `support_ticket_attachments`. A ticket's words, school and raiser cannot be changed (column grant: status only); replies and screenshots are append-only; nothing can be deleted. **Who sees what (RLS):** tenant isolation, narrowed RESTRICTIVE: the principal and school admin see every ticket of their school, **anyone else sees only the tickets they raised** (my choice: a ticket can hold something a colleague should not read; say if every role should see all of the school's); a ticket is raised in one's own name and role and starts open; a reply or screenshot only on a ticket one can see, in one's own name, never as NetCyberAI. The founder reads and answers as `nca_super`. A ticket one cannot see is "not found". **Audit:** `ticket.created`, `ticket.status_changed` (from, to, which side, and `via: reply` when a reply caused it), `ticket.message_added`, `ticket.screenshot_added`: ids and statuses only, never the words (C41). **FileStorage** (`src/lib/storage/`): the interface (`put`, `signedUrl`, `remove`), an in-memory store for tests, and the Supabase implementation on the existing private `documents` bucket (added to the service-role allowlist). A screenshot is PNG, JPEG or WebP judged by its own bytes (an SVG or PDF is refused whatever its name), at most 4 MB (under the host's request limit, so people see the app's own message), at most 5 per ticket, only while the ticket is being worked on; its key is `<schoolId>/tickets/<ticketId>/<uuid>.<ext>` built from the session's school, never from the file's name, and the table refuses a key under another school's prefix; it is handed out only as a 5-minute signed link after its row is read under RLS. The upload checks the session and the declared size before reading any of the body, and the file is stored between two short transactions (plan, store, record), never inside one; if the row cannot be written the file is removed. If the store is not configured (C48: `SUPABASE_SERVICE_ROLE_KEY` is still not set in Vercel) the upload answers 503 and the ticket itself still works. **APIs:** `/api/app/tickets` (list, raise), `/[ticketId]` (detail), `/messages`, `/status`, `/attachments`, `/attachments/[id]`; `/api/control/tickets` (queue, `?status`, `?schoolId`), `/[ticketId]`, `/messages` (with `thenStatus`), `/status`, `/attachments/[id]`. Raise Ticket is always on, so a read-only or suspended school can still ask for help. One person can raise at most 10 tickets and write at most 60 replies an hour. **Ticket rows in the audit log follow the ticket:** a RESTRICTIVE policy `audit_ticket_rows_own_or_office` shows `ticket.%` audit rows to the office and to the person who acted, so a colleague cannot learn from the audit log that a ticket exists or what kind it is. **Not in 4a:** screens (4b); an email to the founder when a ticket arrives (proposed for 4b, through the jobs pipeline). **Review:** an independent review found no leak of ticket content across schools or colleagues and no status bypass; taken from it: session before body and the size bound, the file stored outside the transaction with clean-up, the audit-log policy above, lengths counted as the table counts them (an emoji subject was a 500), Hindi file names kept, the school's direct waiting → in progress move removed, a screenshot refused on a resolved ticket, an invalid filter refused instead of read as "everything", the reply limit, and `check_append_only_grants.py` now reads column-level grants. Not taken: the guard scripts still do not check that RLS is enabled on a new table, or read `grant all`. **Proof:** `test/support/tickets.test.ts` (14 on PGlite, school calls as `app_user`): the table of moves; all 40 pairs refused or allowed by the database; the full walk with its six audit rows and no ticket text in them; refused moves change and audit nothing; reply rules; a teacher or accountant sees only their own, the office the school's, nobody another school's; the founder sees all with school names; raw-SQL attempts (another school, a colleague's name, another role, not open, the platform side, editing, deleting) are refused; validation and the hourly limit; screenshots by bytes, prefix, link only for those who see the ticket, limits, a failure of the store or of the row leaves neither a row nor a file, and the routes answer 401 before reading a body. **Live (2026-10-05, after PR #53 was opened and reviewed):** test database first (recorded as `20261005171906`, the file's name), then production (`20261005172004`). On the test database, as `app_user` in a transaction that rolls back: a teacher raised a ticket and saw it; a colleague saw 0 tickets and 0 ticket audit rows; the principal saw the ticket and its audit row; another school's principal saw nothing; a school's open → resolved, editing the subject, raising into another school and replying as NetCyberAI were all refused; other audit rows stayed readable; on the platform side in progress → closed was refused and `resolved_at` was set on resolve. Catalog snapshot: both databases identical in 9 categories (654 columns, 136 policies, 1,970 table grants, the ticket tables' column grants, 12 functions, 5 triggers, 339 constraints, 181 indexes, 67 RLS flags). **Open:** one local CI run failed once in the unit-test step and passed on the next five without a change; the failing test was not captured (the likeliest cause is the C124 chain test's millisecond clock on PGlite, which the C124 review had noted). |

Never the ticket's words. Retried by the pipeline; one email however often the jobs run. Snapshot: policies, functions, columns, grants and triggers identical on both databases. **Cutover:** `docs/18` §3 was started on "go" on 2026-10-06 (steps 1 and 2 done: production empty, schemas identical in all 19 categories, 2 leads copied with the same fingerprint); it is waiting on Noor's steps 3 and 4. |

The app is started with a clean environment, so no real email key or error-reporting address from the developer's shell can be used by a harness run. Playwright is now a pinned dev dependency. Tap targets under 44 px on Students, the student profile, Compliance and five Control pages are now 44 px. **Noted for P1c-3b, not changed here:** the Control school page still shows developer wording (`is_pilot`, `pilot_allow_outbound`, raw purpose keys); the school dashboard's recent activity shows raw action names. **Why:** screenshots at 390 and 1440 in every PR had depended on a hand-made session; signed-in screens were being shipped with only unit tests behind them, and a broken student profile went unseen. |
| **C134** | Supervisor (2026-10-08), phase P1-c, PR 3b-1: `docs/19` rule 3, Hindi and English; and a new supervisor rule on screenshots. P1c-3b is over 1,500 lines in one piece, so it is split: 3b-1 language and the string check (this row), 3b-2 search and "+ New" (C135), 3b-3 My Day, the remaining API messages and the tighter ModulePage check (C136). | **Screenshot rule (supervisor, from now on).** No full-size PNG screenshots in git. A PR commits compressed WebP only, only of screens it changes, at most about 1.5 MB per PR, under `docs/screenshots/<pr-slug>/`. The harness does this with `--keep <dir> --screens a,b` (WebP through `cwebp`) and fails when the set is over 1.5 MB. **One bar above every page** in `/app` and `/control`, holding the switch English / हिन्दी (each name written in its own language); search and "+ New" join it in 3b-2. **Message files** `src/lib/i18n/messages/en.ts` and `hi.ts` with the same keys; `t()` fills in values as text; client screens ask through `useT()`. **The language belongs to the signed-in person, not the browser.** It is saved on the person's own account: new nullable column `users.ui_language` (check `en`/`hi`; migration `users_ui_language`, additive; written only by the account service for the signed-in user's own row, as the mobile number is). A cookie only saves pages from asking: it holds `<user id>.<language>`, is HTTP-only, lasts twelve hours at most, and a cookie written for anyone else is ignored, so on a shared office computer the next person never inherits the last person's language; every sign-in and sign-out clears it, so a choice made on another device arrives at the next sign-in. **No page ever queries for the language:** with no cookie of their own a person sees English at once, and the shell asks their account once (`GET /api/auth/language`), which writes the cookie; the page is redrawn only if they had chosen Hindi, and the buttons wait until the answer is in. During an impersonation only the cookie changes, never that person's setting. **Applied live before merge (2026-10-08), test database first, then production:** column `users.ui_language text` (nullable) and its check present on both; no row has a value; recorded as `20261008025513` on the test database (the file carries that version) and `20261008025531` on production. Catalog snapshot after: 18 of 19 categories identical on both; the policy category's summary hash differed although every one of the 136 policies is identical one by one. Cause: a fault in `scripts/catalog_snapshot.sql`, which cut keys at 63 characters so long policy keys tied and sorted differently on each run; fixed here (keys as text), not a drift. **A failed switch says so in plain words** where everyone can see it (new catalogue rule `not_saved`, English and Hindi); a sign-in pass that ran out while the page sat open is renewed once before giving up. **`lang` marks only what is in that language:** the shell's own parts (sidebar, top bar, drawer, tools bar, bottom bar). The page inside is left unmarked while its screen is still in English, so a screen reader reads English as English. **In both languages in this PR:** the whole shell: the menu (8 groups, 34 modules, Control's 13 items), role names, account and sign-out, the phone menu and bottom bar, and the switch. Page bodies follow in P1c-4 and P1c-5 (the allowlist below). The Hindi is a first translation; after review of the first draft, the phrases that would mislead were rewritten (the substitute-teacher module, the attendance hint, Curriculum Gap, Academics, and a few bookish words). It stays marked unreviewed (`HINDI_REVIEWED = false`) until the founder approves it; not a blocker (C133). **String check (new CI step 16, `scripts/check_ui_strings.py`).** Finds hard-coded words in the `.tsx` files of `/app`, `/control` and the component folders they use: text between tags including text beside a `{value}`; the attributes a person reads or hears, however quoted; quoted sentences anywhere (a ternary, a toast, a list of options); one-word labels where labels go. A file not on the allowlist may have none. The 86 screens written before the switch are on `scripts/ui_strings_allowlist.json` with the number each still has (1,008 in all). **The list only shrinks:** a file may not go above its number; a lower count must be recorded (`--update`, which never raises a number, never adds a file and never recreates a deleted list); the list is compared with the one the branch started from (its merge base with `origin/main`), so a number raised or a file added by hand fails; a renamed or split file may carry its number across when the old file is gone and the sum has not gone up. A line the check takes for words but is not (a date pattern, a brand name) is marked `// ui-strings: not words (reason)`; the markers are counted in the check's output (none today). The check also fails when `en.ts` and `hi.ts` differ in keys or `{values}`, use a word `docs/19` names as jargon, or contain a line the check cannot read. Its `--self-test` plants 22 shapes of hard-coded words and every way round the ratchet. Limits, stated plainly: it counts, so swapping one hard-coded string for another inside an allowlisted file is not seen; words kept in `.ts` data files or built in variables are not read. **Harness:** switches to Hindi and back with the real button, for the school and for Control, and now fails if it cannot switch back. **Why:** the people who use this are not technical and many read Hindi more easily than English; a screen that writes its own words can only ever speak one language; and on a shared office computer one person's choice must not become the next person's. |

In a school it finds students (name, admission number, roll number, a parent's phone, showing only that phone's last four digits), staff, and tickets by reference or subject. **Which kinds are searched** follows the role and the school's modules: a module switched off is not searched (rule 7); staff are searched by the office only, as the staff list is (C89). **Which rows are found** is decided by row-level security in the caller's own tenant context, never by the API: a teacher finds only students of their own classes and only tickets they raised, and one school never finds another's student, staff member or ticket, even by exact reference. Control searches schools, people and tickets across schools as the founder. At most six of a kind; what is typed is text, never a pattern. "+ New" per role, per module switch and in read-only mode. **Found in review, not changed here (both older than this PR, for the supervisor to schedule):** (1) hiding an action for a read-only or suspended school is on screen only: the add-student and attendance APIs check the role but not the school's access mode or module switch, so `docs/19` rule 7 ("its API returns 403") does not hold for writes yet; (2) Student 360 search matches a parent's phone anywhere in the number from four digits, so a teacher could extend a guess digit by digit for children of their own classes; matching the end of the number only would close it. **Why:** a clerk with a parent at the counter must find the child in two seconds, and the day's common additions must be one tap from anywhere, not a hunt through a menu. |
| **C136** | Supervisor (2026-10-08), phase P1-c, PR 3b-3: `docs/19` rule 2 (My Day), the rest of rule 9, and a tighter rule 1 check. Contract `src/lib/contracts/today-cards.ts`: each module contributes its "today" cards through one provider shape; the home keeps no list of its own. A card carries facts, never sentences and never an invented number: a count (and, where known, a total) computed from the school's own rows inside the person's own tenant context, so row-level security decides what may be counted. A provider with nothing to say returns no card; with no card the screen says "Nothing is waiting for you today" and what to do; a card that cannot be computed is left out and the screen says some cards are missing, never that the day is empty. A module switched off takes its cards away (rule 7). **Cards today** (`src/lib/today/cards.ts`): teacher: "Mark attendance: Class 5-A" per class with how many students are not yet marked, turning to "Attendance saved" when done, and tickets waiting for their reply; office: classes with no attendance saved today, students waiting for consent, fees due today, tickets waiting; principal: the same attendance and consent cards, fees past their due date, tickets waiting, messages held back today (for consent, hours or budget); accountant: fees due today and past due. "Past due" is worked out from the due date, because nothing sets a fee to `overdue` when its date simply passes (the office dashboard's "Fees overdue" tile still reads the stored status and so under-counts; older than this PR, for the supervisor to schedule). Noted for the supervisor: (1) a class teacher who teaches no subject in their own class cannot mark its attendance today; that is the existing policy, older than this PR, and the students policy already treats a class teacher as the class's own; (2) My Day's "today" is the day in India, while the attendance API still takes the UTC date, so between midnight and 5:30 a.m. India time they disagree (also older than this PR). **In the shell:** My Day is the first link of the menu for everyone; for roles without a dashboard it replaces it (in the teacher's bottom bar too), and `/app/dashboard` sends them to My Day instead of a "coming" page. The office keeps its dashboard, one tap from My Day. A teacher's card opens Attendance with the class already chosen (`?class=`). The page and its cards are in English and Hindi. **Error catalogue (rule 9).** The developer-worded refusals of the consent, data-request, document, guardian, guardian-verification, settings and WhatsApp-settings APIs (59 messages in 14 routes, including the ones that passed a thrown error's own wording to the screen) are now rules of the catalogue: `ruleError()` and `accessDenied()` in `src/lib/frameworks/route.ts`. Sixteen new rules in English and Hindi with a test each: `not_found`, `already_linked`, `guardian_not_linked`, `guardian_not_verified`, `two_step_delete`, `only_for_deletion`, `approve_first`, `already_done`, `step_out_of_order`, `code_expired`, `code_wrong`, `code_too_many_tries`, `file_empty`, `not_sent_properly`, `module_off`, `read_only`. No status code changed. A test fails if one of those routes writes its own refusal sentence, or passes on a thrown error's words, again. **ModulePage check, every path (rule 1).** `scripts/check_module_pages.py` now reads each `return` of the page's component and of the view it hands the screen to: every one that returns a screen must return `ModulePage`, a view that is one, or the shared Coming page. A page can no longer show the template to some people and its own layout to others; a return that chooses between two screens in one expression is refused. It fails when it cannot read a component (an arrow function, a return type before the body, a screen built in a helper and returned at the top level). Ten negative cases in `--self-test`. Its first run found one real case, fixed here: the ID card screen drew its loading state outside the template. **Why:** a teacher opening the app at 8 a.m. should see "Mark Class 5-A attendance", not a menu; a refusal should say how to fix it; and one layout only holds if no path can step outside it. |
| **C137** | Supervisor (2026-10-11), phase P1-c, PR 4a: three fixes from the P1c-3b review, and two items moved to P2. | **1. The write guard (rule 7).** Every request under `/api/app` that changes something (POST, PUT, PATCH, DELETE) is now refused when the school's account is paused (suspended) or read-only, or when the module the route belongs to is switched off for the school; before, most write routes checked only the role. The check sits in the one guard every route already calls (`requireAppSession`, which `appRoute` wraps), so a route cannot forget it: `src/lib/frameworks/write-guard.ts`. Which module a route belongs to is written there once, by path (46 write routes today). A write route missing from that table is refused and fails the test, so a new route must say where it belongs before it can change anything. Raise Ticket stays possible in every state. The role remains each route's own check. Refusals are catalogue rules in English and Hindi (`read_only`, `module_off`, new `account_paused`). Where a guardian's records belong: writes to guardians, consent, guardian verification and data requests belong to **Parents**; the student's own record, documents, family and ID card to **Students**; classes, subjects and teaching assignments to **Setup**. Inviting staff and resetting their passwords depend on no module's switch, only on the account's state: every school must be able to bring its people in, whatever its plan (the existing onboarding test caught my first mapping of these to the Staff module, which is not in the standard plan). **Tests** (`test/frameworks/write-guard.test.ts`): the rule; every write route is in the table and goes through the guard; and the real student, attendance and class routes on real Postgres refuse with the right rule and write nothing when the school is read-only, suspended, or has the module off, while a school in good standing is not held up and reading and raising a ticket still work. **2. Phone search.** A parent's phone is matched only as a whole number: ten digits; or `+`, a country code and the number; or the two other ways a whole Indian number is commonly written (a 0 before it, or 91 before it). Part of a number finds nobody, so a number cannot be found by guessing digits. Name and admission-number search are unchanged. Applies to Student 360 search and the shell's global search, which share the code. Negative test: no part of a guardian's number, from either end, finds the student. **3. Fees overdue.** One rule in one place (`src/lib/fees/overdue.ts`): an entry is overdue when it is still pending and its due date has passed by the date in India (an entry already stored as `overdue` counts too; due today is not overdue yet; paid, waived and part-paid never are). Used by the dashboard's tile, its attention item, hero chip and chart, and by My Day's card; the chart's other slice now says "Pending, not yet due", so nothing is counted twice. `/app/fees` is still the module's "coming" page, so there is no list behind the tile yet; when P3 builds it, it uses the same rule. The nightly job that stores `overdue` belongs to P3 Fees and is added to `docs/16` §4. A bulk change that was queued is checked again when its job runs: if the school was paused, made read-only or lost the module in between, the run is marked failed with the reason and nothing is changed. **Not in this PR, for the supervisor to decide:** (i) the guard covers changes only; a switched-off module still answers reads on the routes that never checked (rule 7 says its API returns 403); the table of routes and modules now exists, so a read-side check is a small follow-up. (ii) A part-paid fee past its due date is shown as "part paid", not as overdue, as the brief defines overdue; it could be counted on its unpaid balance once payments are built in P3. My Day's "due today" card counts pending and part-paid entries due today. (iii) A read-only school cannot reset a colleague's password or look up an ID card (the lookup writes a scan log); both count as changes today. **Moved to P2 (`docs/20` §3), not done here:** (a) a class teacher who teaches no subject in their own class cannot mark its attendance; (b) the attendance API uses the UTC date instead of India's. **Why:** hiding a button is not a refusal; a phone number is personal data and must not be discoverable by trial; and a number on a dashboard that is always zero teaches people to ignore the dashboard. |

A tour is a small card in the corner: it never blocks the screen, can be skipped at any step (Escape skips it only when no form, menu or search is using Escape), comes straight after the header in reading order with each step announced to a screen reader, and outlines the part of the page the step is about. Each step was checked against the screen it describes; the Compliance screen's own one-line purpose was corrected with it. No tour on a ticket's sub-pages or on the person's own account page. Control has none. **Remembered per person, on the server.** New column `users.tours_seen text[]` (default empty, at most 100 entries; migration `users_tours_seen`, additive; written only by the account service for the signed-in user's own row). As with the language (C134): a cookie tied to the user id only saves pages from asking, a cookie written for anyone else is ignored, sign-in and sign-out clear it, and no page queries for it. While it is not yet known whether a person has seen a tour, none is shown, so a tour dismissed on another device does not flash back while the app is finding out (a browser that still holds its twelve-hour cookie may show it once more before it catches up). During an impersonation only the cookie changes: the founder never uses up a person's tours. **Applied live before merge (2026-10-11), test database first, then production:** column `users.tours_seen text[] not null default '{}'` and its size check are present on both; no person has an entry yet; recorded as `20261011080039` on the test database (the file carries that version) and `20261011080057` on production; the live site stayed healthy between the two. Drift check after, the same query on both: 656 columns, 341 constraints and 136 policies, with identical hashes for each. **Harness.** Every ordinary screen is photographed as a person who has seen the tours; three screens are opened as a first visit, the tour is skipped with its own button, the page is reloaded without the cookie, and the run fails if the tour comes back. It retries a screen up to twice when the local throwaway database resets a connection. **Why:** a screen that needs a training session is wrong, not the user (`docs/19` §1); and what a person has already been shown must follow the person, not the office computer. |
| **C139** | Supervisor (2026-10-11), phase P1-c, PR 4b-2: `docs/19` rules 7 and 8, empty states that teach and the module switches' sentences. Stacked on 4b-1 (C138). | **Empty states that teach.** One shared component, `TeachEmpty` (`src/components/app-ui/TeachEmpty.tsx`): what is missing, one plain sentence on what to do next, and the button that does it, with the words in the message files in English and Hindi (`empty.<id>.title`, `.do`, `.action`). Eighteen empty states on the school app's lists replace bare lines such as "No guardians linked yet.": the student list (Add student), classes (Add a class), a student's guardians (Add guardian), brothers and sisters (Link a sibling), data requests (Log a request), a class with no active student in Attendance (Open students), staff search with no match (Clear the search), Taught today with nothing assigned, and the record of NetCyberAI's access to the school's data. Where the person's role cannot take the next step, the sentence says who can and there is no button (a teacher on a student's page or in Attendance; Taught today for a teacher). The student list tells three cases apart: a school with no student yet, a search that matches nobody (Clear the search), and nobody waiting for consent. A list that could not be loaded says so and never claims to be empty. The tickets list already had its sentence and button. Control's lists are the founder's and keep their own wording. **What a module being off changes, and for whom (rule 7).** `src/lib/i18n/module-off.ts`: every built module has one plain sentence of its own in English and Hindi (`moduleOff.<id>`), written against what the code does when the module is off (the menu, search, "+ New", My Day and the write guard of C137); a module that is not built yet says so and names the roles whose menu its "Soon" line leaves. The sentence is shown (1) in Control beside every module switch and again in the box that confirms switching one off, and (2) on the school's own Settings page in a new list, "What your school has switched on": each built module with On, Off or Always on and its sentence, the unbuilt ones folded under one line, and a line of its own when the school's account is paused or read-only. Settings is now reached from the office's menu (before, it had no link). Each sentence was checked against the code: it names the menu, search, "New", My Day and the changes the write guard refuses, and none says data is lost. **Who turns the switch.** The school's list is read-only, with a button to raise a ticket. Module switches stay with the founder in Control (C112: a school's entitlements change only there, each change audited with its reason, and the database grants school roles read access only). If the supervisor wants a principal to switch modules from Settings, that is a new decision with a migration (grants, policy, audit), not a wording change. **Why:** an empty list that only says "nothing here" leaves a new clerk stuck; and a switch nobody can predict is a switch nobody dares touch. |
| **C140** | Supervisor decisions (2026-10-11) and P1c-5 part 1 (T5, T7). | **Decisions.** (1) Module switches stay with the founder in Control (C112); a school asks through a ticket. (2) A switched-off module is blocked for reading too: its page shows the "module off" state and its GET APIs answer `module_off`; lists several modules draw on (classes, subjects, staff accounts) stay readable. (3) A part-paid fee past its due date goes to the P3 row of `docs/16`. (4) A read-only school may still reset a colleague's password and look up an ID card; a paused school may not. **T5.** A module the website calls `live` must have its page, API and table (`LIVE_EVIDENCE` in `module-status.ts`, checked in CI), and the website and the app's registry must agree on what is live. The check's first run changed three statuses to what the code shows: Parents is `partial` (its work is on the student's page; it has no screen of its own), Help Center and Raise Ticket are `live`. `ecosystem.ts` takes status labels from `module-status.ts` and states none itself; the homepage is drawn as before. **T7.** The send-gate takes the category from the template and the rate and GST from `message_rate_table` (the row in force on the school's date of sending); no amount is written in the code. **[GAP] No rate is inserted:** Meta's official pricing page (developers.facebook.com, WhatsApp Business Platform pricing, updated 2026-09-30, rates effective 2026-10-01) publishes India's rates only in a rate-card file, which I may not download without permission, so nothing could be verified from the page itself. Until a verified rate is on record a sent message is logged as billable with cost zero and `cost_basis = rate_not_verified`, and Control's new read-only cost view names the gap. A school's own (real) WhatsApp account does not send at an unknown price: such a send is held with the reason "rate not verified" until a rate is on record; only the platform's stand-in, which costs nothing, sends meanwhile. Only amounts costed from the rate table count as money, in the budget and in the view; rows from before this change carry a placeholder and count as "not verified". The same page says Meta charges on delivery, not on sending: `billable` is corrected from delivery receipts when the real provider arrives in P4. **Migration `send_gate_rates`** applied 2026-10-11 to the test database (`20261011085813`, the file's version), then production (`20261011085829`): the three columns and the read grant are on both, the rate table is empty on both, and the same drift query gives identical results (659 columns, 344 constraints, 136 policies, equal grants). **Why:** "off" must mean off; the website must not claim what a school cannot use; and a guessed rate in a cost report is worse than a visible gap. |
| **C141** | Supervisor (2026-10-11), P1c-5 part 2: the provenance standard, docs hygiene, four guides. | **`docs/21_PROVENANCE_STANDARD.md` (E1)** is canonical: what every flag, report and message must be able to show (source, evidence, validation, rule version, AI-assisted or not, approver), stored on the record or in the audit and document tables that exist. No migration; each phase adds its columns. **Docs hygiene, no rule changed.** The second `12_` file is now `22_RECONSTRUCTION_INVESTIGATION.md` (references updated; one comment in an applied migration still names the old path and is left as applied). Six Decision Log ids that appeared twice keep their first row and the second is suffixed `b` (C22b, C23b, C26b, C29b, C30b pointer rows; C99b, the founder's 2026-09-30 build-first instruction). `01` §7's sentence about a price change "scheduled" for October 2026 now says it happened, with the same rule. `22` says transport with GPS is deferred (C23), not banned. **Guides.** Four new Help Center guides in English and Hindi (Compliance, the student's page, Taught today, WhatsApp settings); every school module page now has "Show me how" except the Help Center itself. Control's pages are exempt by rule (the founder's workspace; C140). **[GAP] found while writing the student-page guide, not fixed here:** nothing in the code ever moves a student from `pending_consent` to `active`; recording consent only adds to the consent ledger. Screens and guides already merged say consent does it, and attendance and My Day count only active students. The new guide does not make the claim. Needs a supervisor decision on the rule (which purposes are required; what a withdrawal does) and then a PR with a backfill. **Why:** a number nobody can trace is a number nobody should act on; and two files or two decisions under one number make every reference ambiguous. |
| **C142** | Gap found 2026-10-11: every student is created `pending_consent` and no code path ever set one `active`; recording consent only appended to `consent_ledger`. The attendance roster and My Day count only `active` students, so a student added through the app could never be marked, while tours, empty states, My Day and the Help guides said consent activates the student. | **The status follows the ledger** (`src/lib/consent/student-status.ts`, called by `recordConsentDecision` in the same transaction): the grant that completes the required purposes (PRD §3.5: `core_records`, `operational_communication`; a grant already needs a verified guardian) makes the student `active`, with its own audit row. **Withdrawal (my decision, the documents do not say; supervisor to confirm):** withdrawing a required purpose returns an `active` student to `pending_consent`, and granting it again restores `active`. Reason: Constitution §8 says no student is fully active without consent, the move deletes nothing and is undone by one grant; the cost is that a child who still attends leaves the attendance list until consent is re-granted. Only that withdrawal moves a student back, so one made `active` earlier with no consent on record (the fixture school's students) is not moved by a grant. `inactive` and `transferred` are never moved. **Database** (migration `student_status_follows_consent`): one definition, `student_required_consents_granted()`; a trigger on `students.status` for the app role, because RLS lets a teacher update an own-class student: only the principal or school admin changes a status, none becomes `active` without the required consents, and no app insert starts `active`. **Backfill** in the same migration, one audit row per student, safe to re-run: 0 students on both databases (the test database's 138 active fixture students were seeded active and are not touched; production has no students). Texts made true in English and Hindi; PRD §3.5 states the rule. |
| **C143** | Supervisor (2026-10-11): three decisions after P1-c, and P2 starts (docs/20, slice 5a). | **Decisions.** (1) Consent withdrawal stays as built in C142 (Constitution §8). In P2 the attendance list includes `pending_consent` students with a "consent pending" tag; teachers mark them as usual; no message goes to their families (the send-gate already blocks it). (2) No WhatsApp rate card is downloaded now; rates are recorded at the start of P4, when sending goes live. Not a founder action. (3) The website status changes from the T5 check are accepted. **5a, the phone shell and offline base.** The school app can be installed on a phone (manifest, icons drawn from the logo mark). A service worker, production only and only under `/app`, keeps the app's static files and the last good copy of three pages (home, My Day, Attendance); nothing under `/api` is ever kept; a redirect or an error is never kept. Changes made with no network wait in an outbox on the device (one store per school and person): sent in order, each with its own id so a change sent twice is applied once (the server half arrives with attendance in 5b), a refused change stays visible with its reason and is never dropped silently. One sync line in the shell says saved, sending, waiting, offline or needs attention. Signing out sends what waits, warns if something still waits, then clears the kept pages and the device store. **Guard follow-up.** Routes built on `appRoute` now run the module read check inside their own transaction (one transaction per read, tested). Older routes that call `requireAppSession` directly still use a separate one; they move as they are touched. **Why:** a teacher marks a class where the signal is weakest; the app must not lose or double a mark, and must say plainly what is not yet saved. |
| **C144** | P2 5b (docs/20): attendance on the phone, with the two carried items. | **The day is India's.** Attendance takes "today" from one helper (`src/lib/time/india.ts`), passed into every query; nothing uses the database's UTC date. A mark at 00:30 India time is stored under that India day (tested with a fixed clock). **Who may mark.** `teacher_scope_attendance` now uses `teacher_own_class_ids()`, the rule students already use: a class teacher marks their own class with no subject in it. A mark is saved only for a student who is in that class. **Leave.** A fourth mark, `leave`, with a code (sick, family, school activity, other). **Consent pending.** The roster and My Day include `pending_consent` students, tagged; they are marked as usual and the send-gate holds any message (C143). **Offline.** Submitting goes through the outbox, online or not. The server records each operation id in `client_operations` (own rows only) and answers a repeat with the first answer, so a mark is never doubled. Already marked differently: the first saved mark stays, the person is shown both and may save theirs as a correction with a reason. A submission that arrives after its day ended is saved to no day and stays visible on the phone with that reason. The phone keeps class names, student names and roll numbers for the day only. **Measurement.** `usage_events`: one row per submission (class, day, seconds), no student or staff; read by the principal and school admin only. **Not in this slice:** the school's own leave list and a school setting for the edit window (the defaults of docs/20 §7 apply); an office path to enter marks for a day that has ended (open decision). **Why:** a mark must land once, on the right day, under the right class, and the teacher must always know what is not yet saved. |
| **C145** | P2 5c (docs/20): the timetable, entry and view. | **One row is one period** of one class on one weekday: subject and teacher (`timetable_entries`). **Clash guard.** A teacher cannot be in two classes at once and a class cannot hold two things in one period. The app checks first and names the other entry; two unique constraints refuse what the app missed. **CSV import** (class, day, period, subject, teacher's sign-in email) is all or nothing: with any problem or clash nothing is imported and every line to correct is listed. A line replaces what is saved in its own class slot, so a file may swap two teachers. **Who.** The principal and school admin write (RLS, restrictive). A teacher reads their own periods and their own classes' timetable. Each change is in the audit log. **Periods in a day** is a school setting (default 8, at most 12). **My Day** shows a teacher today's periods in order; it is information and is never counted as work waiting. **Module status:** Timetable is now partly live (the generator, workload and change history stay in P7). **Not decided here:** a module is switched on per school by the founder (C140), so existing schools do not get Timetable until it is switched on in Control; new schools get it with their plan. The academic coordinator reads but does not write. **Why:** Today and the taught-today coverage (5d) need real periods, and a timetable that allows a clash is worse than none. |
| **C146** | Supervisor (2026-10-12), after P2 5a–5c: three decisions and one check. | **(1) Timetable on for every existing school** by a data migration that is safe to run again and leaves a founder's own switch as it is; new schools get it from their plan. **(2) The office's correction path.** A principal or school admin may enter or correct attendance for a past day, with a reason, audited, inside a window that is a school setting (default 7 days, at most 60). A phone's marks that arrive after their day ended are no longer refused inside that window: they become a request in the office's queue (`attendance_correction_requests`). Applying a request fills in the students with no mark for that day and leaves a saved mark alone; dismissing saves nothing; both need a reason and are audited. Older than the window, or for a day to come, a submission is still refused visibly. No message goes to a family about a past day. **(3) The academic coordinator writes the timetable** as well as reading it (policy and routes). **Check:** `scripts/harness/offline-prod.mjs` builds for production and proves the service worker: with the server stopped the Attendance page reopens, takes a submission into the outbox, and sends it once when the server returns. It stops the server because a browser's offline switch does not reach a service worker's own requests. **Migration order:** test database, check it, then production (5c applied both in one step; not repeated). **Why:** a mark made in good faith should reach a person who can decide, not vanish; and offline has to be proven on the build that schools use. |
| **C147** | P2 5d (docs/20, D5): the syllabus map and one-tap "taught today". | **The classwork log is `syllabus_progress`**: one row per class, subject and day, now with the syllabus unit it covered and a lesson note. No second log table. **One tap** (on the next topic of the map, or on a status after typing the topic) writes that row, prepares one Pulse draft linked to it (`pulse_messages.status = 'draft'`, `source_progress_id`, unique), and records one `taught.tap` measurement on the first tap only. A second tap changes the same row and draft. A message made ready goes back to draft when its lesson changes; one already sent is never rewritten. A lesson not taught is never prepared as taught. **Syllabus map** (`syllabus_units`): chapters and topics, typed or pasted one per line; a topic a lesson has used cannot be removed. **Coverage** is taught units over planned units. **Tap coverage** is computed from the source: lessons on the timetable for a class and day against those with a mark, any mark; the office sees the last 7 days. It is per class and day, not per teacher: `usage_events` holds no staff by rule (17 §3b). **Offline:** the tap goes through the outbox; the server's one-row-per-lesson rule makes a repeat harmless. **Teacher scope:** a teacher now reads and writes only their own classes' Pulse messages and syllabus map. **Why:** the teacher does one thing once, and the log, the parents' message and the measure all follow from it. |
| **C148** | P2 (docs/20 5f, D6): the "needs help" queue. | **`pulse_followups`:** one row is one student who needs a follow-up: what kind, a few words why, and the outcome. In P2 a teacher or the office adds it; a parent's reply adds it from P4 (`source`); the outcome feeds Early Warning in P5. One open item per student and kind. **The suggested follow-up is a fixed sentence per kind**, written once in English and Hindi; no AI. **Open → done or not needed, once, with an outcome**; the database holds the same rule; every move is audited; a decided item is not reopened (a new need is a new item). **Order:** as the items arrived. The queue never ranks children. **Who:** those who teach or run the school; a teacher only their own classes (RLS). **Daily Pulse is now partly live** on its own page, so it is switched on for every existing school by the same idempotent migration as Timetable (C146); I applied that decision's rule to this module too, for the supervisor to confirm. Composing the day's message follows homework (5e). **Why:** a child flagged as needing help must end with a recorded answer, not a notification. |
| **C149** | Supervisor (2026-10-12), after #76–#78: three decisions. | **(1)** Daily Pulse switched on for existing schools: confirmed. A module that becomes usable is switched on for existing schools by an idempotent migration that leaves a founder's own switch alone; new schools get it from their plan. **(2)** Taught-tap coverage stays per class and day in the measurement table. A per-teacher view comes in P5, computed from the classwork log and shown only to the principal and that teacher (Constitution: no teacher leaderboard). **(3)** Applying a late-marks request never overwrites a saved mark: confirmed. **Why:** measurement must not become a ranking of people, and a saved mark is changed only by a person who chose to change it. |
| **C150** | P2 5e (docs/20): homework. | **One homework, one assignment per section.** The teacher writes it once (`homework`) and ticks the sections; each section gets its own copy (`homework_targets`) with its own text and due date, checked on its own (`homework_status`: done or not done per student). **Changing the original asks which sections' copies change**; an unticked section keeps the homework as it was given. **Completion** works offline through the outbox; the later answer stands; each submission is audited. **Files:** a photo or a PDF (decided from the file's first bytes, at most 4 MB, three per homework) through `FileStorage`, under the school's own prefix, opened only by a short-lived link after the row is read under RLS. If the store is not available the homework is saved without the file and the screen says so. **Who:** those who teach or run the school; a teacher sets and sees homework only for their own classes (RLS). **Homework is now partly live** and switched on for existing schools (C149 rule). Parents do not receive it until P4. **Why:** copies that silently change after children have taken them home cause disputes; a copy must be what was given unless the teacher says otherwise. |
| **C151** | P2 5f (docs/20): the Pulse draft's words. | **Fixed templates, no AI,** in English, Hindi and Hinglish (`src/lib/pulse/text.ts`): the same lesson always gives the same words. Taught, partly taught and delayed each say what happened; **a lesson not taught produces no message** and takes back one prepared earlier. **The day's homework** for the same class and subject is added, whether set before or after the tap, and follows a later correction. **Three bodies are stored on the message** (`body_en`, `body_hi`, `body_hinglish`) rather than one row per language: one lesson is one message, and the language is chosen per parent when sending exists (P4). **A person may change the words** (kept when homework is set later) and mark the message ready; ready needs words in all three languages (also a database rule). If the lesson itself changes, the words are written again and the message is a draft again, so a message never contradicts the classwork log. **Nothing is sent:** no route or function can mark a message sent, no job is enqueued, and the page says sending starts with WhatsApp. **Why:** the teacher's one tap should leave a message a parent could read as it is, and it must never claim teaching that did not happen. |
| **C152** | P2 5g (docs/20): Homework Recovery v1. | **Who is listed:** a student marked "Not done" for N or more homeworks in the last M school days; both are school settings (3 and 10 by default), and only the school's working days are counted. Homework left unchecked is not evidence and is not counted. **The list is worked out when the screen opens**; only decisions are stored (`recovery_plans`). **The suggested plan is a rule, not AI:** the missed homeworks, oldest first, one a school day from tomorrow. **Approve or dismiss** stores the plan the server worked out (never one sent by the screen), in the decider's own name, audited. A decided student leaves the list and returns only after a newer homework is missed. **Nothing reaches a parent in P2:** an approved plan waits for the message that starts with WhatsApp. **Order:** by name; no score, no ranking; students and parents never see the list. **Module status:** Homework Recovery was *spec v0* (the founder confirms before its phase, 16 §3). It is built here on the supervisor's instruction to complete docs/20, marked partly live and switched on for existing schools (C149 rule); the founder's confirmation of the spec is still to be recorded. **Why:** three missed homeworks is the point where a quiet word helps and a fourth reminder does not. |
| **C153** | Supervisor (2026-10-13), after #79–#81: four decisions. | **(1)** Homework Recovery v1 (rules only, no AI) is confirmed under the founder's standing instruction that every module is built (C104); it stays partly live. **(2)** Three language bodies on one Pulse message: confirmed; the language is chosen per parent at sending (P4). **(3)** Teacher Copilot (5h) is built now on a stand-in `AIProvider` and is off for every school until the founder supplies a key and a monthly limit. Inputs are syllabus, lesson and class level only; no student name or personal data goes to the model. AI-drafted remarks about a student wait for the consent-purpose decision (brief O6). Uploads in the deployed app depend on the deferred cutover (docs/18 §0), so this is not a founder task; the "saved without its file" behaviour stays. **Why:** AI may draft for a teacher about a topic; it may not be given a child. |
| **C154** | P2 5h (docs/20): Teacher Copilot v1. | **`AIProvider`** is the one door to a model (as `FileStorage` is to files). What goes through it is a `DraftRequest`: kind, class level, subject, chapter, topic, and a length. It has no field that could hold a person, and it is built from the school's syllabus map, never from text someone typed. **Three kinds:** lesson plan, worksheet, the day's message. No remark (C153). **Off unless three things are true** for a school: the module is switched on for it, the founder has set its monthly limit (`limits.monthly_tokens` on the entitlement), and a provider is configured. The screen says which is missing. No migration switches a school on. **Only the stand-in provider exists**; it never answers in a production build. The real provider is added and tested when there is a key. **Metering:** `ai_usage` holds who, what kind, tokens and when; never the prompt or the draft. A school at its limit is refused before anything is asked. **Nothing is kept until the teacher approves:** an approved lesson plan or worksheet is saved as the teacher left it (`teaching_notes`), marked AI-assisted, under the approver's name (docs/21); the message kind goes into the lesson's Pulse draft, which is still not sent. **Module status:** foundation, not live: built and tested, switched on nowhere. **Why:** a draft saves a teacher time only if the teacher stays the author. |
| **C155** | P2 closed against docs/20 (§10 there). | Every acceptance line is tested except those listed open in docs/20 §10: a real-phone install and offline open; a browser test of sign-out; the teacher's same-day edit window as a setting; a real AI provider; Hindi review. None blocks P3. The P3 plan is `docs/23_P3_FEES_PLAN.md` (22 is already the reconstruction investigation; two files under one number were removed in C141). |
| **C156** | P3 plan: `docs/23_P3_FEES_PLAN.md`. | Seven slices, in order: heads and structures; raising dues with concessions and RTE tagging; counter payments and receipts; overdue (the nightly job, and part-paid past due on the unpaid balance); the sibling concession and family dues; the payment link on a stand-in `PaymentProvider` (Razorpay behind the same interface when the founder's test keys exist); fee recovery, email reminders and the clearance gate. **Rules for all of P3:** whole paise only; `gross − concession = net` and `net − payments = balance ≥ 0` on every line, checked by one query in the tests and nightly; payments append-only, corrected by a reversal; a gateway payment applied once; every concession a person's decision; fee rules versioned, never overwritten; no fee data for teachers; the follow-up list is of families, for the office only. **Not in P3:** late fees (needs the founder's real policy), the RTE claim desk (P7), WhatsApp reminders (P4). **Why:** a school trusts a fee system only if every rupee can be traced from the structure to the receipt. |
| **C157** | P3 6a (docs/23): fee heads and structures. | **Money is whole paise** (`src/lib/fees/money.ts`): rupees typed by a person are parsed without floating point; anything that is not a plain amount is refused. **A fee rule is never overwritten** (E4). The app adds a head or retires one (its name never changes), and adds a **new version** of a class's structure from a date inside the session; the version in force on a date is the latest that began on or before it. The app's database role can no longer update or delete a structure or its items, or delete a head. **Teachers and coordinators read no fee rule:** a restrictive read policy on the three tables (before this, any staff role in the school could read them). **Fees is now partly live** and switched on for existing schools (C149 rule); the page says dues, payments and receipts are not built yet. The plan (docs/23 §1) said the fee tables were empty; the test database holds fixture rows, corrected there. **Why:** dues already raised must keep the amounts they were raised with, so the rule they came from has to stay readable for ever. |
| **C158** | Supervisor (2026-10-14): decisions on docs/23 §5. | **(1) Late fee** is built as a school setting, default OFF; rules and amounts are configuration, never constants (it comes with the nightly job, 6d). **(2)** A payment goes to the oldest due first by default; the office may pick a different due when recording a payment, audited. **(3)** A percentage concession is rounded half up to the paisa, once: confirmed. **(4)** Reminders by email in P3, WhatsApp from P4: confirmed. **(5)** No default sibling rule; each school sets its own, applied only from confirmed family links, with approval (C116). **(6)** The file name `docs/23_P3_FEES_PLAN.md` stands. Also: the fee head's "mandatory" flag is editable on the Fees page. Payments run on the stand-in `PaymentProvider` until the founder's Razorpay test keys exist; the webhook is idempotent and payments are append-only. |
| **C159** | P3 6b (docs/23): raising dues, concessions, RTE tagging. | **A ledger line now says what it is for and how its amount was reached:** head, period, gross, concession (with its detail), net. The database refuses a line that does not add up, and a second line for the same student, head and period. This closes the gap recorded in the fee engine migration. **Raising dues** for a class and month takes the structure in force on the first of that month; quarters and halves are counted from the session's first month; a one-time head is due once. It is safe to run twice. **Active students only:** a student waiting for consent is invisible to the accountant (the consent gate), so including them would make the result depend on who pressed the button; raising the month again once they are active adds their lines. **Concessions:** a finance role proposes, with a reason; the principal or the school admin approves, and never their own proposal. Nothing applies until approval, only to dues raised afterwards; a concession is ended, never deleted. Percentages first (each on the gross, half up to the paisa), then fixed amounts; together never more than the gross. **RTE** (D4, tagging only): the principal or the school admin marks the child; from then on the heads every child pays ("mandatory") are raised and conceded in full as `rte`, so what RTE covers can be counted later. An optional head, such as transport, is not covered. **Reads closed:** dues, payments and concessions were readable by any staff role in the school; now only by the principal, the school admin and the accountant (dues also by a parent, their own children's). **Why:** a rupee conceded must be as traceable as a rupee paid. |
| **C160** | P3 6c (docs/23): payments at the counter, receipts, reversals; and a correction to earlier migrations. | **One press, one receipt.** Money received is put against dues oldest first, or against the dues the office picks (recorded as `chosen` and audited, C158). Each due touched gets a payment row; together they are one numbered receipt (`receipts`; the number comes from the numbering framework, format a school setting). The screen sends an id with each press, so a double tap or a retry is one receipt and one credit. A student's payment may cover the whole family's dues. **Payments stay append-only.** A wrong receipt is reversed whole, with a reason, by the principal or the school admin: each payment gets an opposite row naming it; the receipt stays, marked reversed; its number is never used again. **The database holds the rules itself:** a payment larger than what is still owed on its due is refused; a reversal must be the exact opposite of one unreversed payment of the same due; a receipt changes only by being reversed, once. More than is owed is refused (no advance in this slice). **Reconciliation** (`reconcile`): one query gives net, paid and balance for the school and lists any due paid out of range, any line that does not add up and any receipt that does not match its payments; the Fees page shows the three totals and says so if there is a problem. **Correction (found by a test in this slice):** both databases give the app's role every right on a new table by default, so the P2 and P3 migrations that said `grant select, insert` narrowed nothing; the role could also update and delete those tables. RLS still applied and the app issues no such statement, but the tables were not append-only at the grant level as the PRs said. The intended rights are now set by revoking the rest, and `test/db/grants.test.ts` pins the exact rights of each table. **Why:** in a fee system the rule has to live where no screen can forget it. |
| **C161** | Supervisor (2026-10-15): security hardening of database rights, found by checking both databases. | **Fault.** On about 44 older tables the Supabase roles `anon` and `authenticated` held SELECT, INSERT, UPDATE, DELETE and TRUNCATE. RLS blocks their rows, but TRUNCATE is not covered by RLS. **Root cause:** the default privileges of schema public give every new table, sequence and function to anon, authenticated and service_role, and every new table to `app_user` with all four rights (the cause of C160 too). The app never uses anon or authenticated for tables; supabase-js is used for sign-in and file storage only. **Fix, one migration:** every right of anon and authenticated on every table, sequence and function in public is revoked; five trigger functions executable by PUBLIC are closed; the default privileges of `postgres` in public no longer give anything to anon, authenticated or app_user (the same is attempted for `supabase_admin` and is refused on Supabase, where `postgres` is not its member; nothing in public is made by it). `app_user` also loses TRUNCATE, REFERENCES and TRIGGER everywhere, and two more tables are brought down to what their own migrations said (`message_rate_table`: read only; `staff_invitations`: no delete). **Rule from now on:** a migration grants its table's rights to `app_user` explicitly, and says `revoke all on function ... from public` for a new function. **Kept true by a test:** `test/db/grants.test.ts` pins the app role's rights on every public table (81), fails on a table that is not listed, and fails if anon, authenticated or PUBLIC can do anything to any table, sequence or function. The harness database now starts with Supabase's own default privileges, so the fault is reproduced there and the test fails without the migration. **Not changed:** `service_role` (the server's secret key role, which bypasses RLS by design) and `nca_super` keep their rights; PostgreSQL's own built-in default that a new function is executable by PUBLIC is not changed globally (it would reach extensions), so the rule and the test carry it. **Why:** RLS is the second lock; the first is that a role with no business in a table holds no right to it. |
| **C162** | Supervisor (2026-10-15): four fee decisions. | **1.** Dues are raised for active and pending_consent students, not inactive or transferred: a child owes fees before the parent's consent is recorded; consent governs messages, which the send-gate blocks. The consent gate on `students` is unchanged; the fee desk reads the billing fields only (name, class, family, status, RTE tag) through `fee_billing_students()`, for the three finance roles of the caller's school. **2.** Each fee head has a "covered by RTE" flag; a new head takes its mandatory flag, then the two are set apart. RTE applies to covered heads. **3.** The accountant proposes concessions and cannot approve them or reverse receipts: confirmed. **4.** No advance payments for now: confirmed. | 1 and 2 change the earlier C159 choices (active only; RTE follows mandatory). A definer function, not a wider policy, so the accountant still sees no other field of a waiting child. |
| **C163** | P3 6d: overdue, part-paid past due, late fee (docs/23). | **One status rule** (`fee_status_for`): past its date with anything still owed is overdue, part paid or not; by India's date. Not fining the past is my choice, to keep a switch-on from fining every old due overnight; say if the school should choose. Between midnight and the job the screens go by the date, so they never wait for the job. |
| **C164** | Supervisor (2026-10-16): decisions on C163, and the platform role's rights. | **Confirmed:** a late fee never fines past dues; "off" is amount 0, flat, once (for now); the new fee chart breakdown. **Rights:** `nca_super` (bypasses RLS) held insert, select, update and delete on every new table through its own default privileges, whatever the migration said. On 20 school-data tables it is now the same as the app's role (`fee_status_runs`: insert and select); its defaults are removed, so a migration names every role it grants to. `test/db/grants.test.ts` pins every role on every table: app_user, nca_super, app_backend, audit_writer, service_role; a role not named holds nothing. | Third instance of the C160 fault. No statement in `src/` used the removed rights. `service_role` keeps everything on 78 tables (pinned as it is); whether to take that away is an open decision. |

(3) Bharat Connect recommended for Noor to explore with BOUs (automatic, any bank, school's own account). (4) NetCyberAI never holds fee money (that would make it a payment aggregator). (5) Razorpay KYC no longer blocks P3. (6) C168/C169 choices confirmed: a signed notice with another amount is credited as owed, the excess goes to the refund list; a rule concession counts from the first of its month; switching the sibling rule off ends its concessions on dues raised later, and earlier dues keep theirs. A message's state moves forward only (sent → delivered → read, or sent → failed), in the code and by a trigger; a held message never becomes sent; what a message was never changes. **Money follows delivery:** a real account's message is priced when sent, billable when delivered, free if it failed; the budget counts delivered and on-its-way. **Communication** lists the school's messages of 14 days with their state, for the principal and school admin. | A parent's reply is counted and not kept until replies are threaded (7f): `webhook_events` is not where a family's words belong. | **C184** | P4 slice 7c: templates with versions and two approvals. | **The words live in code** (`src/lib/whatsapp/templates.ts`): one entry per version, in English and Hindi, with its variables, Meta's category, the consent purpose and how long it stays worth sending. **Every body begins `{{school}}: `** (C183): tested over the whole registry, checked by `definitionProblem`, and held by a database constraint; the gate fills `{{school}}` from the school's own row, over anything a caller passes. **The database** holds one row per version and language with **our approval** (status, by whom, when) and **Meta's** (its id and decision) apart; a version's words never change (trigger). **The gate** sends the newest version we approved, in the language asked for (English when there is none); a real account also needs Meta's approval; a missing variable refuses the send before any provider is asked. **The log** keeps the version, the language and the values. **Control → Message templates**: bring in the code's versions, approve or reject, record Meta's decision. So absence notices wait until the founder approves the words in Control. Words that are no longer the code's cannot be approved there. Hindi is unreviewed. |
| **C185** | P4 slice 7d: the outbox. | A message is **queued** in the caller's transaction (one log row, one `whatsapp.send` job) and sent by the job runner, where every rule is read again at that moment. The rules a person wants to hear about at once (pilot, consent, budget, words not approved, a value missing) are also read when queueing: such a message is recorded as held and never queued. **Quiet hours hold, they do not drop:** the message goes when they end, unless by then it is older than its template says it stays worth sending; then it is not sent and says why. **A provider failure** is tried again (3 attempts; 2 then 10 minutes apart). **Never twice:** the row is marked as being sent and committed before the provider is asked, and the provider is asked outside any transaction; an interrupted send is recorded as failed and is not repeated. The job queue takes a run-at time. A school session may enqueue `whatsapp.send` for its own school only. Absence notices now go through the outbox. | An interrupted send may in fact have reached the parent; it is shown as failed because nobody knows, and sending again could message a parent twice. A queued message has said nothing yet, so its version and values are settled when it is sent and fixed from then on. `sendWhatsAppMessage` (send now, quiet hours block) stays for callers that must not wait; nothing uses it now. |
| **C186** | P4 slice 7e: Meta's Cloud API adapter, dormant, and the platform's WhatsApp credentials. | **The adapter** (`src/lib/whatsapp/meta.ts`) is built from Meta's documentation as read on 2026-10-13 (URLs in the file and the PR): a template send with named parameters; the webhook's first check; the `X-Hub-Signature-256` signature over the raw body with the App Secret; status and text notices; a read of the phone number as the connection test. Tests use recorded examples in Meta's documented shape; no network call is made. **Credentials** follow the payment pattern with their own key, `MESSAGING_CREDENTIALS_KEY`: AES-256-GCM in the app, bound to the phone number id, one row, write-only, no right for any school role. Without the key, saving is refused in plain words. **The switch** is in Control → WhatsApp account: saved switched off; on only after a connection test that passed (the database holds that too); a failed test or a replaced account switches it off. **The gate** uses the real account only when it is switched on, the server holds the key, the school is not a test school, a verified rate is on record and Meta has approved the template; otherwise the stand-in. The adapter itself refuses a test school (the second lock). Only the platform role can read the credentials, so real sends go through the outbox. | A school's own number (`school_whatsapp_configs`, Vault) is no longer read by the gate at all; the tables stay. A real account now never sends without a verified rate, so `rate_not_verified` no longer arises for real sends. Refused notices are kept at most 100 an hour, so strangers cannot fill the table through the public address. Meta's formats are as documented, not as observed: the first real send is the real test, to the founder's own number first. |
| **C187** | P4 slice 7f: parents' replies, threaded. | **Whose message it is.** One platform number serves every school, so a parent's message is given to a school only when its number is a guardian's on record there; if in several schools, to the one that last wrote to that number in 30 days, if exactly one did. The principal and school admin see all; a teacher only those of guardians of their own classes (RLS); nobody else. **Replies:** free text only inside the 24 hours the parent's own message opened, checked when asked and again when sent; queued like every message; never billable. **STOP** (also रोकें, बंद) stops WhatsApp from that school to that number at once, held as `blocked_consent`; **WhatsApp only**: the consent ledger is not touched (C183). The parent is told once, in words from the code. The office lifts it when the parent asks, with a reason. **Numbers** are written one way (`+`, country code, number); a ten-digit number with no country code is Indian. The gate now normalises the number before any provider sees it. | A reply that the provider fails is not retried: the member of staff sees it failed and can send it again. An earlier draft let a teacher's reply slip past STOP because teachers could not read opt-outs; found by its test, fixed in the policy. |
| **C188** | P4 slice 7g: fees on WhatsApp. | **The reminder:** a family is reminded once a day on **one** channel. If none is queued, email, exactly as in C179. Never "paid". **The receipt:** queued at the ledger door, where a receipt is issued, so only after the money is verified (by the school, the bank's own line, or a gateway's signed notice). All three are templates from the code, queued through the gate. A message that cannot be queued never undoes what it is about (a savepoint). | **C189** | P4 slice 7h: Daily Pulse is sent, and parents' answers are counted. | **Sending:** only a message a person marked ready, and only when a person presses "Send to parents" (C116). The day's words travel in one template, `daily_pulse`, on one line, after the school's name. **Answers:** "1" (understood) or "2" (needs help), in a few plain forms. An answer is tied to a Pulse only when the number was sent the Pulse of exactly one child in the last 24 hours; otherwise it stays a message in the school's thread and nothing is recorded. "Needs help" opens a follow-up for that child (source `parent_reply`), in that child's class, so the existing rules show it to that child's teacher and the office only. | English only for now: the app does not know which language each family reads; the Hindi and Hinglish words stay on the screen for the teacher. | **C190** | Supervisor (2026-11-03): decisions on C183–C189. | **Approved:** a school's own number is not read; ten-digit numbers are Indian; reminders stay on email until WhatsApp is proven; the Pulse template is `utility`; the templates page's WABA id and the send address's phone-number id are both correct. **Changed, and built here:** (1) An interrupted send is **uncertain**, not failed, and is never retried. (2) **A number known to several schools, none of which wrote in the last 24 hours:** the parent is asked "Which school is your message about?" with the schools as quick replies (a numbered list above three). At its due time, still open, it becomes the principal's and shows on the principal's My Day. | **C198-A** | Supervisor (2026-10-14): founder pricing, boards and the public website. | **Pricing (founder range ₹15–₹50 per student; supervisor split):** Starter ₹15, Growth ₹30, Compass ₹50 per enrolled student per month; yearly = ten months for twelve (₹150, ₹300, ₹500). (3) **Bulk certificates** from a CSV in the free studio: one PDF, numbered in sequence, 200 rows (50 on a phone); the product does it from the school's records. | **C200** | P5 8a: the C198 follow-ups (docs/26). | **Rule:** (1) Nobody notes, reassigns or closes a complaint about themselves, the principal included (the app and two new restrictive policies). One about the principal is recorded by the school admin; overdue, it becomes the school admin's (or another principal's if there is no admin), shows on the admin's My Day and not on the principal's, and the parent is told it went to "the school office" (new template `complaint_escalated_office`). The parent is told only when it really changes hands.
