The principal still reads a complaint about themselves, and is told on the screen why they cannot change it. A teacher known only from the timetable cannot read that class's guardians, so the office books for them. |
| **C201** | P5 8b: staff leave (docs/26). | **Rule:** A member of staff asks for whole days of leave on My account. The principal or the school admin decides, and never the person who asked: the principal's own request is the school admin's to decide, and the other way round. The office can record leave for someone who phoned in; it is approved at once in that office person's name. Only the person and the office can read a request (row level security), and the platform role holds no right on the table. A day is on leave only while a request covering it is approved. Staff is partly live and switched on for every school. | The database holds the rules, so no screen or script can step round them: a check (decided_by is never the person), policies (a decision or a withdrawal is written in one's own name, a decision only from the office), and a trigger (what was asked never changes; refused or withdrawn is final; no overlap while waiting or approved). Three columns were added to the plan's list (withdrawn_by, withdrawn_at, withdrawn_note) so a withdrawal has who and when and never overwrites the decision. The person withdraws while it waits or before the first day; after that the office cancels, with a note. Audit rows of leave are narrowed to the office and the person who acted, and carry no kind or reason, because every role of a school could otherwise read them. Leave is never counted by person or ordered into most and least; nothing in Copilot, recognition or the Teacher Impact screens reads it (a test holds this). Limits: 60 days a request (database), the office may record leave that began up to 31 days ago, anyone up to 366 days ahead. No balances, no half days. |
| **C202** | P5 8c: Proxy Auto (docs/26). | **Rule:** For a date, every period the timetable gives a teacher on approved leave is listed with the school's free teachers in one order: teaches the subject, then fewest periods that day, then fewest covers that week, then name. A person assigns with one tap and can undo; the app assigns nothing and sends nothing. A period is covered once and a teacher covers one class a period, held by two unique indexes. The principal, school admin and academic coordinator arrange cover; a teacher reads only covers where they are the substitute or the absent teacher. | A teacher is a user, as in the timetable. The academic coordinator arranges cover but may not read leave, so the database gives the arranging roles one fact only (which teachers are away on a day), and the coordinator only for today and the 31 days cover is arranged for. A cover's audit rows follow the cover, as leave's do. A cover widens nothing a substitute can read. When leave is cancelled after a cover, the cover stays until a person undoes it (shown as no longer needed): the app does not undo by itself. The order of names belongs to one decision; no total of covers by person is kept. |
| **C203** | P5 8d: school facts and the principal's dashboard (docs/26). | **Rule:** C203: School facts and the principal's dashboard (8d). src/lib/insights holds one set of 34 school facts. The office's dashboard is drawn only from these facts for today, 7 or 30 days (?range=): groups of tiles, an attention list, charts with a table, 'Why this?' everywhere, English and Hindi, and an audited CSV of what is on screen. No migration. **8d-2:** complaints (open, past due) and meetings (coming) now open their rows, so every fact with a number has a list; three more facts: leave requests waiting, staff on leave today (office only) and periods without cover today (also the coordinator), counts only, never a name or an order of people. Present rate, rule version 1: present or late out of every mark saved; leave is marked and is not an absence. A student on leave now counts as marked. A fact's roles mirror row level security narrowed to the module's roles; a role that may not read gets no fact, never a zero; 'not set up' is its own state. Students waiting for consent open Admissions; the Students screen takes ?status=. Five tables have no role policy, so for those the registry and the routes are the only guard: recorded as an open decision because it needs a migration. |
| **C204** | P5 8e: Control billing plans (docs/26). | **Rule:** Plans are settings (C204). A plan's name, price, period, grace days, modules and limits are rows of `plans`, typed in Control; the code names no plan and no price. The plan a new school gets is the platform setting `billing.default_plan`. Applying a plan gives a school exactly that plan's modules. A school has one subscription row (plan, paid until) and payments recorded by hand, which are never edited: a wrong one is corrected by a new row that names it. Each night just after midnight in India a job moves a billed school past its paid-until day into grace for the plan's days, then read-only, audited, and the school reads a dated line on its own screens. | The nightly check tells its own work from a person's by what is stored: it remembers what it last set and acts only while the school's access is still exactly that. A suspended school, a mode set by hand, or full access given back by hand all stand until a payment is recorded. Paid-until is always the latest covers-until among payments that still count, so one rule serves payments and corrections. No amount, reference or note goes into the audit log, because a school can read its own audit rows. The Control billing routes re-check on each request that the account is still live NetCyberAI staff. The seeded plan has no price and no period; its 15 grace days are the builder's choice until the founder sets his own. |
| **C205** | P5 8f: Control messaging cost by month (docs/26). | **Rule:** C205. Control's messaging cost is shown by month of India's calendar (?month=YYYY-MM; this month by default, never a later one). A message belongs to the India month it was logged in. Month spend keeps its C197 meaning for every month: delivered, billable messages at the price recorded from the rate table when each was sent. One set of SQL in src/lib/whatsapp/cost.ts feeds a school's own budget line, Control's list of schools, its table by category and its six months. A school is 'near' its limit at four fifths or more of it in delivered spend, and 'at or over' at all of it. The list downloads as a CSV with the rows on the screen and leaves an audit row without amounts. | One rule in one place, so the two halves of the page cannot disagree (docs/26 rule 2); the page says in one sentence what each figure counts. The month is a half-open range from India's midnight, so the existing index on (school_id, created_at) is used and 00:10 on the 1st is the new month even though it is still the old one in UTC. The limit shown for a past month is the one set now, and the page says so: a limit is not kept month by month and this slice has no migration. The export refuses a bad or future month rather than send a file for a different month. Screen and file are laid out by one function, so they cannot drift. No tour or guide: Control pages carry none (C140). |
| **C206** | P5 8g: Control monitoring (docs/26). | **Rule:** C206. Control's Monitoring page is one read of counts and ages, as the platform role, of everything that can stop without anyone noticing: jobs (dead, stuck, late), refused delivery notices, messages that were uncertain, failed or held back (by live school and reason), replies held for "which school?", the nightly fee run and billing check for yesterday in India, templates, and the health check. It shows school names and job types and never a parent's or child's name, a number, an address or a message's words. A job's error, a message's reason and a notice's outcome are shown only after cutContacts has cut out emails, anything that looks like a phone number, WhatsApp message ids and quoted values. The health check is one function that /api/health and the page both call. A section says "Needs a look" only for what waits on NetCyberAI. | The loader reads no column that holds family data, so privacy does not rest on the scrub alone; the scrub is for texts code and providers write. A school is expected to have a fee run for yesterday only if it had a due on record before yesterday began: dues raised later were not there to be run, and flagging them would be a false alarm. The billing check leaves nothing when no school is past due, so its own job row for that night is what says it ran. "Yesterday" and not today, as the plan says: between midnight and the run, today would be a false alarm. Holds for no consent or quiet hours, jobs waiting for another try and Hindi versions not reviewed are counted and explained but do not raise the flag, so the flag keeps its meaning. Dead jobs are listed before jobs waiting for a retry so an outage cannot push them off the list. No tour and no guide: Control pages carry none (C140). |
| **C207** | P5 8h: Reports (docs/26). | **Rule:** Reports is a fixed list of eight tables by role, with no builder, no schedule and no PDF. Finance (principal, school admin, accountant): daily collection; dues and defaulters. Principal, school admin and academic coordinator: attendance by class; absentees; homework coverage. Principal and school admin only: Daily Pulse coverage; messages by state; staff leave. A report runs in the reader's own session for India's dates, both ends included, at most 366 days (today, 7 days, 30 days or two dates); dues is about this moment. Its rows come from the same module function the dashboard number calls, and one function makes every total row from the rows, so a total equals its rows and the dashboard's number for the same days. A report that is not the role's, or whose module is off, is absent from the list and refused by its address. The CSV is the same run written by the same column rules, and each download writes one audit row (report, days, row count, language; never the rows). Reports becomes partly live and is switched on for every school. | One rule in one place: a report groups a module's own list by day, class or family and writes no second definition. Roles mirror the school facts: the tables' row level security narrowed to the module's roles; fees never sit beside learning. Daily Pulse coverage is not given to the coordinator because the Daily Pulse module and its facts are not that role's (17 section 5). Rows come in the order of a date, a class or a name, never of a count; dues by the longest wait, never the largest amount; the leave list shows no kind and no reason. Attendance, Daily Pulse messages and the message log have no role policy in the database, so for those the report's own role list refuses before any query runs; the test names each such pair. A report that would read more than 20,000 source rows is refused in plain words rather than cut short. No new table, policy or grant. |
| **C208** | P5 8i: School Health Auditor v1 (docs/26). | **Rule:** School Health Auditor v1 has twelve fixed rules (rule version 1). Each check passes, needs attention (with a count and the screen that fixes it), or is not set up. A check that cannot run is "could not be checked". The result is "N of M checks pass": M is every check that is not "not set up", and a check that could not run counts in M but not in N. Check-ups are run on demand by the principal or the school admin, kept one row a day in school_health_runs, and a past day never changes. No language model is used. | Each count calls the school fact or the module's own function, so the auditor and the dashboard cannot disagree (rule 2). A "not set up" check is left out of M so a school is not graded on what it has not started (rule 1). A check that could not run stays in M so a failure never reads as a pass. A switched-off module makes its checks "not set up". The database holds N and M to the stored results, so a made-up score cannot be written. Counts and app addresses only: no names, so nothing ranks staff (rule 4). |
| **C209** | P5 8j: Early Warning v1 (docs/26). | **Rule:** Early Warning v1 is live for staff only. A person presses "Check now" (principal, school admin or academic coordinator); the check opens a flag for an active student with a reason, brings an open flag's evidence up to date, and closes it when no reason remains. Reasons are attendance (in school on fewer than 75 of every 100 days over 30 days, from ten counted days, leave days left out; all three numbers are school settings), homework (exactly Homework Recovery's list) and an open needs-help follow-up. Each flag stores its reasons, evidence and rule version 1. A teacher reads the flags of their own classes and writes none. What a person did is an append-only log. Nothing is sent to a parent: notified_parent is held at false by the database and nothing reads the school's switch. | The tables are early_warning_flags and early_warning_actions, not at_risk_*: the secrets check refuses "sk_" anywhere under src, and "at_risk_" holds it. The check is a person's tap, not a nightly job (docs/26 rule 7), so a flag closes at the next check. A flag's class follows the child at each check and is held to the child's own class by a trigger, because a teacher's reading rests on it. The platform role holds no right on either table. Fees are never a reason; students waiting for consent are not flagged. Lists are by class then name, never by count or rate. |
| **C210** | P5 close-out (supervisor decisions, 2026-10-14, on the open items of #140). | **Rule:** (1) `attendance`, `pulse_messages`, `pulse_response_log` and `communication_log` gain RESTRICTIVE role policies matching the app's role lists, tested as `app_user` for each role. (2) A subject teacher reads the names of guardians booked on their own PTM slots through a narrow definer function, and nothing more; general guardian access is not widened. (3) The academic coordinator sees whether a teacher is away or present, never the kind or reason of leave (`26` rule 4). (4) One-person office: the principal decides the school admin's leave; the principal's own leave goes to the school admin; with no school admin, the principal records it, approved and audit-marked. (5) Early Warning runs nightly and the School Health Auditor weekly through the job queue, as the school's office would see it (the school's own row level security, no person; the rows say the nightly check or the weekly check-up made them); a scheduled job is queued only once the deployed runner handles its type; an Auditor My Day card; a class-teacher setting on the Classes screen. (6) Dashboard lists refresh after an action; Control monitoring masks staff emails; cancelling leave releases that person's covers from today on and tells the covering teacher on My Day. The P4 modules' public status notes are not changed here (founder, 2026-10-14: the website is the supervisor's). | Each closes an open decision or gap reported with P5. Running the checks in the school's own context keeps the platform role without rights on flags, leave or check-ups, and makes the scheduled numbers equal what "Check now" shows. |
| **C211** | P6 plan (`27_P6_EXAMS_PLAN.md`). | **Plan:** six slices: 9a boards and the grading scheme builder; 9b exams and marks entry; 9c report cards, publish, Personal Best and Most Improved; 9d Curriculum Gap Detector; 9e Learning Genome v1; 9f Early Warning v2. Decision Log numbers C213 to C218 are reserved for them in that order (C212 is the pricing slice, which comes first). **Founder, 2026-10-14:** schools record their board; grading is a scheme builder (bands, pass marks, theory, practical and internal components, terms, report card layout); presets only for CBSE and CISCE, each copied from the board's official document with its URL; no invented state board preset: Control adds one from the board's official circular when a school from that board signs; P6 does not touch the public website. **Rules:** no grade table in code but the two presets; no ranking of named children: Personal Best and Most Improved compare a child only with their own earlier exams (supervisor, 2026-10-14), so the competitive banded tier of `recognition.ts` is not built; a published result is frozen and only the principal reopens it, with a reason; fees never a learning signal; nothing new to a parent; rules only, no model; every threshold a setting. | The CBSE document grades Class X by octiles of all the Board's passed candidates, which a school cannot compute and which ranks children, so its preset offers the Class IX absolute scale the document tells schools to award, copied as printed (it leaves marks 41 to 44 in no band, and the builder shows the gap). The CISCE Regulations give grades 1 to 9 and the pass marks but no mark ranges, so that preset has none. `recognition.ts` is locked (`01` §6) and is imported, never edited. |
| **C212** | Pricing per active student (founder decisions, 2026-10-14). | **Rule:** a plan has a price a month and a price a year per active student, the year proposed as ten months (two months free); a school's subscription records which period it chose. Three plans are seeded as data and are editable in Control: Essential (students and families, attendance, fees with UPI, receipts and reminders, notices and Communication, Daily Pulse, the teacher app), Growth (Essential with exams and report cards, PTM, events, complaints, Parents, staff leave and Proxy Auto, the principal dashboard and reports), Premium (Growth with Parent AI, Early Warning, School Health Auditor, Learning Genome, Curriculum Gap, Personal Best). The principal dashboard, staff leave and Personal Best are plan features inside a module, asked by the screens and routes that own them; a plan with every working module, and a school with no subscription (the plan for new schools), has them all. Per school, in Control, each with a reason, dates and an audit row: a negotiated per-student price, complimentary modules with an end date, a discount percent, a minimum of billed students. Amount due = max(active students, minimum) × the price for the period − the discount, one function, in paise. A plan field for the WhatsApp messages included per student a month stays empty until Meta's rates are entered. No price in code: the prices exist only as data. | Founder's pricing. Modules the founder did not name: help, tickets, setup and compliance are in every plan (DPDP duties); recovery, academics, homework, timetable, dashboard (My Day) and staff accounts are in Essential as the teacher app; documents in Growth and Premium; admissions, payroll, teacher-copilot, career, twin and automation in none yet. 8e's single price and period are retired, not dropped, until this code is live. The public website is not changed here. |
| **C213** | P6 9a: boards and the grading scheme builder (docs/27). | **Rule:** Exams becomes partial, with the grading scheme screen as its own. Board presets are rows seeded by the migration (docs/27 rule 1 now says so); the ISC preset also grades SUPW and Community Service A to E, as its Regulations do. grading_schemes is append-only and versioned. One percent(), grade() and passed() in src/lib/exams/grading.ts. A percent is rounded half up by a scheme setting (whole number or one decimal place) before its grade and pass are found. A gap copied from a preset is shown with the document's own note. | The founder's brief asked for the presets as data, and a test then finds no grade table in the app's code. Versions keep every saved scheme and a later change rewrites nothing. A rounding setting is needed because integer bands such as 81-90 and 91-100 leave 90.5 in no band; the school chooses it, and it is shown where it is used. Partial lets a plan give the module, so the READ and WRITE guards can name it. |
| **C214** | P6 9b: exams and marks entry (docs/27). | **Rule:** C214: 9b exams and marks entry. Each exam has papers (one class and subject each, with the scheme's components and their maxima, and its chapters as syllabus unit ids). Marks are stored per paper, component and child, with score, absent or exempt. The trigger holds 0 <= score <= the component's maximum, one decimal place, and an open exam. One rule, may_enter_marks(), decides who may enter, in both the policies and the app. A paper is 'entered' when every child on the roll has a mark, absent or exempt. The old marks are backfilled into single-component papers. The platform role loses its rights on exams and marks. | docs/27 §3 rules 2, 3, 5 and 6, and row 9b. Absent is never zero. No ranking. The accountant reads no mark. The backfill stops rather than guess. A paper with half its marks entered shows no percent rather than a misleading one. |
| **C215** | P6 9c: report cards, publish, Personal Best and Most Improved (docs/27). | **Rule:** An exam is published per class into exam_publications (a new version each time) and keeps a copy of the grading scheme in force. A trigger freezes that class's marks, papers and remarks until only the principal reopens it, with a reason, audited. Report cards are drawn in the browser through src/lib/pdf from one text model laid out by the kept scheme. Personal Best and Most Improved are computed at publish from recognition.ts against the child's own earlier published exams, kept with the publication, and shown only while the plan has 'personal-best'. | The database holds the freeze for every writer. A publication is fixed when made: the scheme, the progress lines and attendance up to the publish day do not move later. Doing the progress at publish under the office's reading rights means the class teacher's PDF says the same as the office's. One text model means the PDF and the screen cannot disagree, and a test can read the exact words printed. The competitive tier of recognition.ts is never read (C211). |
| **C216** | P6 9d: Curriculum Gap Detector (docs/27). | **Rule:** Curriculum Gap Detector v1. On every read, each unit of a class and subject's syllabus map gets one of five states by one rule (src/lib/curriculum-gap/rules.ts), with the days it rests on and a count of each state; nothing is stored. The office and the coordinator see every class, a teacher their own, through a database reader with its own access rule (curriculum_gap_units). The module is 'partial' for TEACHING roles, scoped to own classes. | docs/27 row 9d and section 5. A fifth state, 'not taught yet', is added so that every unit appears once: the plan's four states leave out units the class has not reached. Taught means status 'taught', the same rule the syllabus map already uses. The reader has its own rule because the table policy lets a teacher see only lessons they recorded, which would put units in the wrong state for a class teacher or a second teacher of a subject. A teacher's access is required by the slice ('a teacher their own'), so the module roles change from LEARNING_AI to TEACHING. |
| **C217** | P6 9e: Learning Genome v1 (docs/27). | **Rule:** Learning Genome v1 is computed when it is read and shown to staff only: the office, the coordinator, and a teacher for their own classes. Exams count once their paper is marked entered, until 9c's publish. Strengths and gaps use seven settings of 'learning-genome' and compare the child only with their own previous exam, their own overall in that exam, and their own replies and homework. Chapter replies are read through genome_chapter_replies, a narrow definer reader (counts and days only) answered to the office, the coordinator and a teacher of the child's class (lead, at integration: C210 keeps the coordinator out of the raw Daily Pulse tables). | docs/27 row 9e and rules 2, 3, 6, 7, 8 and 9. One rule in one place, through the class result's shown(). Row level security decides who reads which child, and nothing new is stored or sent. |
| **C218** | P6 9f: Early Warning v2, the marks signal (docs/27). | **Rule:** Early Warning rule version 2 adds the reason 'marks'. In a child's latest exam whose papers for their class are all entered (stand-in for 'published' until C215), their own percent fell by at least the setting's points (default 15) against their own previous exam, or is below the scheme's pass mark in at least the setting's number of subjects (default 2). Exams are ordered by when sat (papers' latest date, else the day made). Percents use the class result's rule and rounding. The database admits 'marks' only on rule version 2 or later and requires it to name its exam; version 1 flags stay as they are. | docs/27 row 9f and section 3: one rule in one place, every threshold a setting, a child compared only with themselves, fees never a signal, nothing to a parent. Ordering by date sat rather than date made keeps a school that makes its exams in advance, or corrects an old mark, from comparing the wrong exams. |
| **C218-A** | Supervisor (2026-10-14): P5 close-out, pricing and P6 merged (#141, #143–#145, #147–#151, after #152). | **Merged** in stack order once both databases matched (every schema hash equal on test and production; same 18 migrations in the same order) and the stack's top joined with main passed tsc and 2,037 unit tests. #152 renamed admission numbers in a website test that the new grade-table guard read as CBSE grades. The message-log bridge has closed itself (`early_warning.nightly` recorded). **Correction:** the plans are **Starter, Growth and Compass** (C198-A, the founder's names, published on the website); C212 seeded `essential`/`premium` by mistake, and the next slice renames them. **Answers:** (1) an exam counts once it is **published**, as docs/27 says; (2) the accountant's "classes not marked" and a teacher's message totals get database policies, not only the app's role list; a teacher's counts covering only their own classes is accepted; (3) Documents in Growth; recovery, academics, homework and timetable in Starter as the teacher app; Teacher Copilot in Compass (metered drafting); admissions and payroll are placed when built; `standard` is for test schools only, a real school is put on Starter, Growth or Compass at onboarding; (4) Personal Best ignores attendance and compares across sessions: accepted; (5) teachers see colleagues' lesson dates and counts for their own classes, without notes: accepted; (6) the free tools keep their editable gap-free scale (the CBSE preset copies the board's printed table, with its 41–44 gap). **Also next:** only the principal and school admin may change their school's row, and only its profile columns; the class-teacher migration skipped test-first: never again. Website module status updated by the supervisor. Claude Code rows continue from C219. |
| **C219** | P6 close-out (supervisor, C218-A). | **Rule:** The plans are Starter, Growth and Compass (renamed in place, audited). Billing counts enrolled students. Early Warning and the Genome count an exam only while it is published. The five dashboard pairs are refused by the database through reads_attendance_days() and reads_school_message_totals(). Only the principal and school admin update their school's name and address; everything else goes through Control (column grants, a restrictive policy and a trigger). The board is set only in Control. The bridge and the old price columns are dropped. Co-scholastic grades are recorded and frozen with the publication. A card's attendance is kept with the publication. Curriculum Gap shows one session at a time. Teacher Copilot is in Compass. 'standard' is test-only, the default plan for new schools moves to Starter, and a school with no subscription keeps every feature. | Supervisor answers in C218-A and the founder's plan names in C198-A. Making the plan test-only with a flag keeps plan names out of code and triggers. Moving the default to Starter was needed so onboarding never subscribes a real school to standard. Keeping every feature for a school with no subscription preserves today's behaviour for schools that predate subscriptions. |

The supervisor and Claude do every step they can; Noor is asked only for inputs that cannot exist without him (for example Razorpay at P3). ## Part 5 — Migrations location (raised as a condition on the Stage 3 approval)

**Decision:** `db/schema.sql` and `db/rls_policies.sql` are the
**locked reference baseline** — what the system looked like when this
was decided, kept byte-for-byte stable so it stays a verifiable point
of comparison. Every actual schema change from that baseline forward
is a numbered file under `supabase/migrations/` (Supabase's own
convention — what `supabase db reset` replays). Nobody edits
`db/schema.sql` directly once migrations exist; a change is a new
migration file, never a diff to the baseline.

Recorded in `03_ARCHITECTURE_PLAIN.md` and applied in
`08_EXECUTION_BIBLE.md` Prompt A, whose four new migrations (A1, C14,
C15, C16) are the first real content this split produces.

---

## Part 6 — What's still genuinely open

The consolidated, canonical list lives in
[`00_START_HERE.md`](00_START_HERE.md). Summarized here for this
document's own completeness:

- Pulse comprehension **response-rate target** (Amendment 3 changed
  the metric; no target carries over from the old open-rate figure) —
  needs 30 days of real-school data.
- `12_RECONCILED_PLATFORM_FEATURE_MAP.md` — confirmed absent, this
  document is its practical replacement.
- The "CTO Recommendation" blueprint — confirmed absent, never
  committed to this repo.
- Current-year CPI figure and official source; current Form 6 filing
  deadline and official field list.
- WhatsApp interactive button character limit and current India
  per-message billing rules — verify at build time.
- Real fee concession types from an actual school.
- DPDP legal basis for parent-facing at-risk dispatch.
- OCR provider selection.
- Final plan pricing in ₹.
- ID card print format and workflow.
- Legal review of `/privacy` and `/terms` — still blocking for launch.
- `SUPABASE_SERVICE_ROLE_KEY` not set in Vercel (Preview or Production) — blocks document upload (Prompt F) and invite-by-email (Prompt E) in the real deployed app; see Decision Log C48. Operator action: copy the real key from the Supabase dashboard into Vercel's env vars.
